# The M2 visual parity audit

What a WoW model is made of, what the legacy OpenGL viewport does with each part, what the
Unity renderer does with it today, and what a viewer sees when the two differ.

This document reports. It changes no rendering behaviour and recommends no tuning. Every
status below is backed by a path and a line number that was read, or by a count taken from
the model files themselves; where neither was possible the status is `unknown` and the
document says what would settle it.

## What was measured, and how

**The baseline is this branch**, cut from `develop` at the commit that merged the Unity M2
lighting work. "Unity" below means the C# and shaders in `Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets`
as they stand here; "legacy" means the C++ OpenGL viewport under `Source`.

Two kinds of evidence were used, and they answer different questions.

- **Reading both renderers.** Ten passes over the two codebases, one per visual domain, each
  then re-checked against its own citations by a second reading whose job was to overturn it.
  This answers *what the code does*. A feature counts as supported only when something applies
  it to the picture: parsing a value and never consuming it is `partial` at best.
- **Sweeping the client's own models.** A new read-only switch, `-m2inspect`, reads a model's
  M2 header and reports what visual systems the file actually declares. Sweeping 8,009 models
  named by the client's file listing turns "which model should I test this against" from a
  guess into a query, and turns "does this matter" into a percentage.

Nothing was judged by eye, no reference images were used, and no rendering code was changed.

## The short version

The Unity renderer reads **13 of the M2 header's array fields**. The legacy viewport reads
essentially all of them. Everything below follows from that gap.

The three systems that account for most of the visible difference, in order:

These six were re-read and confirmed by hand against the cited
lines before being published here, because the ranking below is a synthesis of the
domain reports rather than one of the individually verified table rows. Two were
corrected in the process: the alpha-key mechanism is narrower than first stated, and
a claim about by-path asset fetching was first dropped, then restored once the
call was found under a different name than the one first searched for.

1. **Animated material tracks: UV transforms absent entirely, colour/transparency tracks collapsed to one sample at t=0** — M2Parser.cs:55-68 declares no offset for the 0x60 texture-transform array or its 0x98 lookup, and ReadVisibilityTracks (Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/Wow/M2Parser.cs:318-350) reduces every colour track to one bool + one float and every weight track to one float, so every scroll, swirl, pulse, blink and tint on the model is a still image; worse, a batch whose alpha reads 0 at t=0 is not hidden but deleted (WmvModelBuilder.cs:585-586 `continue`s before the triangle set is added at :592) and can never come back.
2. **Particles and ribbons are not implemented at all** — A grep for particle/ribbon/attachment/ModelLight/ModelCamera/ModelEvent across Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts returns exactly one incidental comment (Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:1728) and no code, and M2Parser's header-offset block (Wow/M2Parser.cs:55-68) reads none of those arrays -- so on the boss-class models the flames, motes, breath jets and weapon trails that make up a large share of the silhouette are simply absent.
3. **Alpha-key cutout cannot discard on the commonest single-texture case** — For a batch with one texture the builder writes `_AlphaMode` as `plan.AlphaMode == 1 ? 1 : 0` (WmvModelBuilder.cs:1534). Alpha mode 0 is what `Combiners_Opaque` resolves to (WmvModelBuilder.cs:1152), and that is the combiner an alpha-keyed cutout batch normally carries, since the key comes from the blend mode rather than the combiner. With `_AlphaMode` 0 the shader's alpha keeps its `fixed a = 1.0` default (WmvOpaque.shader:365-371), so the `clip` at :375 can never fire — even though the keyword is enabled (WmvModelBuilder.cs:1315) and the texture's alpha was correctly kept (`mode != Opaque` makes `alphaIsRead` true, :619-622). Hair cards, fur flaps and foliage draw as opaque rectangles, and cast slab shadows to match. A batch whose combiner does resolve to alpha mode 1 keys correctly, so this is a subset, not every cutout.
4. **The UNLIT render flag is ignored; fullbright is inferred from blend mode instead** — Wow/M2Model.cs:151 is the only occurrence of `.Unlit` anywhere under Assets/Scripts, and _Emissive is set true only inside the two additive cases of ApplyBlendMode (WmvModelBuilder.cs:1251 and :1261, consumed at :1305), so a self-lit eye, rune or lantern that is opaque or alpha-keyed is shaded down to the rig's 0.265 ambient floor (WmvOpaque.shader:96) and reads as a dark painted patch instead of a light source.
5. **Submesh Level word dropped: index starts wrap silently past 65535** — Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:80 reads Level and nothing ever uses it, while BuildTriangles indexes with the bare 16-bit start (Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:138), so any skin profile holding more than 65535 indices -- the large models the in-game reference set is made of -- draws whole submeshes from the wrong triangles, and the parser's own range checks (Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:89-93) pass because a wrapped start is a small in-range number.
6. **A model without an `SFID` chunk fails the whole load, although a by-path route exists** — WmvMain.cs:295-297 gives up with "nothing to render" when the skin-profile chunk is absent, so such a model shows nothing rather than degrading. The plumbing to do better is already on both sides: the player can ask for an asset by path (`RequestAsset` at WmvIpcClient.cs:385) and the host answers it (`UnityAssetAccess::readByPath` at Source/wowmodelviewer/UnityAssetAccess.cpp:164), and the legacy viewport uses exactly that route with a `<name>NN.skin` name fallback (Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1527-1529). In the sweep every one of the 7,598 readable models carried `SFID`, so this is a robustness gap for older clients rather than an everyday one.

## How common each visual system actually is

Percentages are the share of swept models whose own header declares the system, so they are
a measure of how often a missing feature will actually be missed.

7598 models swept (29 more read as encrypted noise and rejected by a sanity bound). Counts come from each file's own M2 header, read by `-m2inspect`.

## The parity table

One row per feature. `legacy` and `unity` columns give the file and line where the renderer
*parses* the data and where it *applies* it; `none` means the search found nothing. Statuses:
`supported`, `partial`, `missing`, `out of scope`, `unknown`. The last column names the
implementation stage a fix belongs to (see the roadmap).

### Geometry and the skin profile

This domain covers everything between the raw M2/SKIN bytes and the triangles that reach the screen: vertex records, the two-level index indirection, submeshes, draw batches, geoset selection, bounds and the coordinate-system change. The headline finding is that the Unity path is broadly faithful and in several places stricter than the legacy C++ (every index hop is range-checked, bone weights are renormalised, the second UV set is read as floats rather than reinterpreted ints), but it drops one field the legacy code goes out of its way to reconstruct: the submesh Level word that carries the high bits of indexStart. Legacy sidesteps the 16-bit overflow by recomputing istart cumulatively into a uint32 (WoWModel.cpp:1601-1606, reason stated at modelheaders.h:239); Unity reads Level (M2SkinParser.cs:80) and then indexes with the bare wrapped IndexStart (:138), so any model whose skin holds more than 65535 indices draws scrambled geometry past that boundary, silently. Two further divergences are visible on ordinary models: batch ordering within a render queue is left to Unity where legacy sorts explicitly by blend mode then submesh index, and the batch-visibility tracks are sampled once at time 0 instead of being re-evaluated per frame. The M2 vertex carries no colour at all -- provable from the 48-byte stride, which both parsers account for exactly with no spare field -- so anything colour-like on a batch comes from the colors[] array, which Unity reads only as a visibility gate and never as a tint. Framing differs cosmetically: legacy frames a straight-on front elevation over all vertices, Unity a three-quarter view over only the batches it actually built.

| feature | M2 source | legacy parse | legacy render | Unity parse | Unity render | status | visible symptom | stage |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Vertex array (positions) | MD20 header vertices M2Array at 0x3C; 48-byte M2Vertex, pos = float3 at offset 0 | wow/WoWModel.cpp:583 (memcpy of header.nVertices * sizeof(ModelVertex) into rawVertices/origVertices); struct at wow/modelheaders.h:198 | wow/WoWModel.cpp:598 (vertices[i] = ov_it->pos), emitted per index at wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:760 | Scripts/Wow/M2Parser.cs:181 (seek 0x3C), :183 (RequireArray stride 48), :476 (three floats) | Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:503 (ConvertPosition), :531 (mesh.vertices = positions) | supported | None. Both read the same 48-byte record and upload every model vertex once; the Unity mesh shares one vertex array across all submeshes exactly as the legacy index buffer does. | none |
| Normals | M2Vertex normal = float3 at offset 20 (after pos 12 + weights 4 + bones 4) | wow/modelheaders.h:202 (ModelVertex::normal), read with the vertex block at wow/WoWModel.cpp:583 | wow/WoWModel.cpp:599 (normals[i] = glm::normalize(ov_it->normal)); emitted at wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:758 / :781 | Scripts/Wow/M2Parser.cs:481 | Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:505 (ConvertNormal), :532 (mesh.normals); conversion at Scripts/Wow/WowCoordinateConverter.cs:45 | supported | No visible difference on well-formed files. One asymmetry: legacy normalises every normal on load (WoWModel.cpp:599) while WowCoordinateConverter.ConvertNormal only permutes and negates components (WowCoordinateConverter.cs:45-50), so a file shipping a non-unit normal would light slightly brighter or darker in Unity than in the OpenGL viewport. | none |
| UV set 0 | M2Vertex texcoord0 = float2 at offset 32 | wow/modelheaders.h:203 (ModelVertex::texcoords) | wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:759 and :782 (glTexCoord2fv on origVertices[a].texcoords); in the combiner path wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:707 then :718 | Scripts/Wow/M2Parser.cs:482 | Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:508 (ConvertTexCoord), :533 (mesh.uv); V flipped once at Scripts/Wow/WowCoordinateConverter.cs:90-94; sampled at Resources/WmvOpaque.shader:314 | supported | None. Unity flips V once in the converter because a BLP's rows are uploaded flipped; the -wmvFlipV switch (WmvModelBuilder.cs:509) exists to prove that decision rather than to guess at it. | none |
| UV set 1 | M2Vertex texcoord1 = float2 at offset 40 (the last 8 bytes of the 48-byte record) | wow/modelheaders.h:204 -- declared as 'int unk1, unk2; // always 0,0 so this is probably unused', i.e. the second UV pair is present in the struct but MISTYPED as two ints | wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:709-710 (reinterpret_cast<const float*>(&ov.unk1/&ov.unk2)) then :715-716, ONLY on the multi-texture combiner path; the plain fixed-function path (ModelRenderPass.cpp:759/:782) never emits it | Scripts/Wow/M2Parser.cs:483 (read as a proper WowVec2) | Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:511-513, :536 (mesh.uv2); shader input at Resources/WmvOpaque.shader:79, passed through at :315, selected at :345 when _Unit1UV == 1 | supported | None for unit 1. Both renderers route the second texture unit to UV set 1 when the material's vertex-shader name says T2, and to a generated sphere map when it says Env. | none |
| Vertex colours in the M2 vertex | There is no colour in the M2 vertex. The 48-byte record is exactly pos(12) + weights(4) + boneIndices(4) + normal(12) + texcoord0(8) + texcoord1(8) = 48, with no bytes left over | wow/modelheaders.h:198-205 -- the whole ModelVertex struct; sizeof is used directly as the stride at wow/WoWModel.cpp:584 | none (there is no per-vertex colour to render). Per-BATCH colour comes from the M2 colors[] array instead: wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:396-435 (ocol/ecol, GL_EMISSION) and :570 (glColor4fv) | Scripts/Wow/M2Parser.cs:31 (VertexStride = 48) and :474-484, which accounts for all 48 bytes with no colour field; struct at Scripts/Wow/M2Model.cs:79-89 | none | out of scope | Nothing is missing at the vertex level -- proven by the stride: both parsers account for all 48 bytes and neither has a spare field. Anything that looks like 'vertex colour' on an M2 is the per-batch colors[] entry, and THAT is missing in Unity: legacy multiplies the batch's RGB into the emissive/primary colour every frame (ModelRenderPass.cpp:428-434, :570) while Unity reads the same entry only as a yes/no visibility gate (WmvModelBuilder.cs:1393-1408) and never tints the material. A model whose glow or eye colour is authored as a colors[] tint renders untinted (plain texture colour) in Unity. | B&#8209;animated&#8209;material |
| Bone weights and bone indices | M2Vertex weights = 4 bytes at offset 12, boneIndices = 4 bytes at offset 16. The indices are DIRECT indices into the bone array (no lookup table) | wow/modelheaders.h:200-201 | wow/WoWModel.cpp:2242-2262 -- CPU skinning: skips weight 0, clamps an index >= bones.size() (:2253-2258), accumulates bones[bi].mat * pos scaled by weight/255.0 with NO renormalisation after a dropped influence | Scripts/Wow/M2Parser.cs:477-480 | Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:437-478 (BuildBoneWeights), :704 (mesh.boneWeights), :709-712 (bindposes), :714-717 (SkinnedMeshRenderer) | supported | None on well-formed data. On malformed data the two differ: legacy leaves the surviving weights summing to less than 1 after dropping an out-of-range influence (WoWModel.cpp:2261 divides by a fixed 255), which drags that vertex toward the origin; Unity renormalises (WmvModelBuilder.cs:470-473) and binds a wholly unweighted vertex to bone 0 (:465-466). Unity's behaviour is the more correct of the two. | none |
| Skin profile file (SFID) -- how many exist and which one is loaded | SFID chunk: an array of FileDataIDs, header.nViews of them for the LOD levels, then further ids for other purposes | wow/WoWModel.cpp:557-574 -- reads exactly header.nViews ids into skinFileIDs and explicitly abandons the rest at :571 | wow/WoWModel.cpp:811-819 (setLOD(0)); wow/WoWModel.cpp:1494-1546 (setLOD opens skinFileIDs[index]) | Scripts/Wow/M2Parser.cs:104 (ReadIdChunk 'SFID', whole chunk); header nViews read separately into SkinProfileCount at :178-179 | Scripts/WmvMain.cs:295-301 -- requests SkinFileDataIDs[0] and nothing else | supported | None: both load profile 0 and neither exposes an LOD switch, so both draw the highest-detail mesh. Two dead spots worth knowing about: Unity parses SkinProfileCount (M2Parser.cs:179) and never reads it, and legacy's own bound check is broken -- WoWModel.cpp:1499 writes 'int numSkinFiles = sizeof(skinFileIDs);', taking sizeof of a std::vector object (a constant, typically 32) rather than .size(). Harmless while the only caller passes 0, but any future LOD selector would clamp against a meaningless number. | none |
| Submeshes (skin sections) | SKIN submeshes array at 0x1C, 48-byte records: id, level, vertexStart, vertexCount, indexStart, indexCount, bone counts, centre/sort positions, radius | wow/modelheaders.h:225-237 (ModelGeoset) widened by ModelGeosetHD at :240-283; built at wow/WoWModel.cpp:1601-1617 | wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:695 (model->geosets[geoIndex]) then the draw loops at :703 / :755 / :778 | Scripts/Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:71-99 (stride 48 at :21) | Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:589-593 (one Unity submesh per batch's submesh), :673-676 (mesh.subMeshCount / SetTriangles) | supported | None for the fields both read. Unity parses only id/level/vertexStart/vertexCount/indexStart/indexCount and says so (M2SkinParser.cs:85); legacy additionally keeps nSkinnedBones/StartBones/rootBone/nBones/BoundingBox/radius (modelheaders.h:251-259) but uses none of them in the draw path either. | none |
| Submesh 'Level' field (high bits of indexStart) | SKIN submesh record, uint16 at offset 2, immediately after id | not read as such -- ModelGeoset.id is a uint32 covering both id and level (wow/modelheaders.h:226), and the level half is masked away by ModelGeosetHD's id(geo.id & 0x7FFF) at :252 | wow/WoWModel.cpp:1601-1606 -- legacy sidesteps the field entirely by recomputing istart cumulatively (istart = running sum of icount) into a uint32, for the reason stated at wow/modelheaders.h:239: 'same as ModelGeoset but with a uint32 as istart, to handle index > 65535 (present in HD models)' | Scripts/Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:80 (s.Level = c.ReadUInt16()) -- stored on M2Submesh at Scripts/Wow/M2Model.cs:322 | none -- Scripts/Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:138 indexes skin.Triangles with the bare 16-bit submesh.IndexStart | **missing** | On any model whose skin profile holds more than 65535 triangle indices -- large bosses and high-poly creatures -- every submesh past the 64K boundary reads its triangles from the wrong place: IndexStart has wrapped and Unity has thrown away the high half. Those parts of the model draw as scrambled shards, or as a duplicate of geometry that belongs elsewhere, while the legacy viewport draws them correctly. The parser's own range check (M2SkinParser.cs:90-93) does NOT catch it, because a wrapped IndexStart is a small number that still lands inside the array -- so the failure is silent, with no log line. | A&#8209;surface&#8209;material |
| Draw batches (texture units) -> one Unity submesh each | SKIN batches array at 0x24, 24-byte records: flags, priorityPlane, shaderId, skinSectionIndex, geosetIndex, colorIndex, materialIndex, materialLayer, textureCount and four combo indices | wow/modelheaders.h:290-307 (ModelTexUnit); one ModelRenderPass per unit at wow/WoWModel.cpp:1653-1655, pushed at :1964 | wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:693-787 (render), one draw per pass | Scripts/Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:101-123 (stride 24 at :22) | Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:570-667 (loop), one triangle set + one Material per batch, :673-676 | supported | None structurally. Both make one draw per batch, and both duplicate a submesh's triangles when two batches target the same submesh (a base layer plus an overlay), so a two-layer material composites the same way. Unity's field order matches legacy's byte for byte -- Unity's SubmeshIndex is legacy's ModelTexUnit::op (the value handed to ModelRenderPass as geoIndex at WoWModel.cpp:1655), and Unity's GeosetIndex is legacy's op2. | none |
| Draw-batch ordering / sort | SKIN batch array order, plus batch.priorityPlane and material blend mode | wow/WoWModel.cpp:1964 (rawPasses in file order) | wow/WoWModel.cpp:1968 (std::sort with sortPasses) and the comparator at wow/WoWModel.cpp:2011-2018: blend mode first, then submesh index, with specialTex as the tiebreak within one submesh | Scripts/Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:101-123 (file order preserved) | no explicit sort. Order is left to Unity, steered only by the per-material render queue set at Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:1211 (Geometry), :1229 (AlphaTest) and :1240/:1249/:1259/:1269/:1278/:1287 (Transparent), applied at :1319 | partial | Opaque before alpha-tested before blended is reproduced by the queue assignment, so the coarse ordering matches. What is not reproduced is the ordering WITHIN a queue: legacy forces a deterministic submesh-index order among same-blend passes (WoWModel.cpp:2016), while Unity's submeshes all sit at the same renderer and the same bounds centre, so their relative order is whatever Unity picks. On a model with several overlapping additive or alpha-blended layers on the same body part -- a glow over a cape, a rune sheet over armour -- the layers can composite in a different order than in the OpenGL viewport, which reads as a halo drawn under instead of over, or a translucent piece popping in front of the piece it should sit behind. | A&#8209;surface&#8209;material |
| Batch priorityPlane | SKIN batch record, int8 at offset 1 | wow/modelheaders.h:294 (ModelTexUnit::priorityPlane) | none -- the only reference is a diagnostic log line at wow/WoWModel.cpp:1934, and sortPasses (WoWModel.cpp:2011-2018) never consults it | Scripts/Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:107; field at Scripts/Wow/M2Model.cs:331 | none | **missing** | Both renderers ignore it, so this is not a Unity regression -- but it is a shared gap. The game uses priorityPlane to force a transparent layer in front of or behind another regardless of depth; without it, models that stack several blended planes (spell effects, layered wings, banner cloth) can show the wrong layer on top in BOTH viewports. | A&#8209;surface&#8209;material |
| Geoset (submesh id) selection from the displayed variant | SKIN submesh id (group * 100 + variant), masked to 15 bits; the SET of switched-on numbers comes from the client database's creature geoset data, not from the M2 | wow/WoWModel.cpp:1607 (default display = (id == 0)); the selected set arrives via app/animcontrol.cpp:1561-1562 (SetSkin -> setCreatureGeosetData) | wow/WoWModel.cpp:2839-2875 (setCreatureGeosetData: showGeoset(i, cgd.count(id) > 0) for every id in [1, geomax), :2869-2874); enforced per pass at wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:387 | Scripts/Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:79 (submesh id); set received over IPC and adopted at Scripts/WmvMain.cs:1295-1315, sourced from app/UnityAssetAccess.cpp:221-232 -> app/animcontrol.cpp:1468-1486 | Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:299-304 (GeosetVisible), :597-600 and :673-676 at build, :1034-1054 (ApplyGeosets on a later variant change) | partial | With a creature selection reported, the two agree: id 0 always drawn, everything else drawn only if the set names it. They diverge when the host reports NOTHING. Legacy's default is display = (id == 0) (WoWModel.cpp:1607), so an unselected model shows only its id-0 submeshes; Unity's GeosetVisible returns true for every submesh when geosets is null (WmvModelBuilder.cs:301-302). On a model with several mutually exclusive variants per group -- a raw character model with 13 hairstyles, or a creature with alternative horns -- Unity draws them ALL at once, which reads as a garbled overlapping blob where the legacy viewport shows a clean (if bald) base mesh. | F&#8209;characters&#8209;equipment |
| Geoset visibility override for legacy-storage creature models | n/a -- a viewer-side rule keyed on storage backend and model path | wow/WoWModel.cpp:1638-1641 (storage == MPQ, not a character model, name starts with 'creature/') | wow/WoWModel.cpp:1642-1643 -- forces display = true on every submesh, overriding the id == 0 default | none | none | **missing** | On a legacy-storage creature model whose baked look lives in non-zero geosets -- the ones carrying hair, beards, ears or held instruments -- the Unity renderer shows the stripped base mesh: bald, earless, missing the drum kit or microphone that the OpenGL viewport draws. Unity never learns about the override because it only ever sees the geoset SET over IPC, and the override is applied on the legacy side of the wire after that set is consulted. | F&#8209;characters&#8209;equipment |
| Batches hidden at rest by an animated track (colors[] alpha, texture_weights[]) | MD20 colors array at 0x48 (RGB track + alpha track, 40 bytes each) selected by batch.colorIndex; texture_weights at 0x58 reached through texture_weight_combos at 0x90 | wow/WoWModel.cpp:1805 (pass->color = Tex[j].colorIndex), :1807 (pass->opacity = transLookup[...]) | wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:396-435 (colour + its opacity track) and :438-463 (transparency track), gated at :468-469 -- both evaluated EVERY FRAME at model->animtime / model->anim | Scripts/Wow/M2Parser.cs:317-345 (ReadVisibilityTracks) with the single-sample read at :383-406 -- animation 0's first key only | Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:1371-1410 (BatchIsVisible) evaluated once at build, :560-587 (skip loop), with an all-hidden escape hatch at :565-568 | partial | A batch whose alpha is keyed to zero at rest is correctly withheld by both. The difference is over time: legacy re-tests the track every frame, so an eye glow that blinks on a global sequence appears and disappears; Unity samples once at time 0 of animation 0 and freezes that answer for the life of the model. A glow keyed off at t=0 never lights up, and one keyed on at t=0 stays lit forever instead of pulsing. The same single sample also means the RGB/alpha values are used only as a yes/no test and never as a tint or a fade -- see the vertex-colour row. | B&#8209;animated&#8209;material |
| Model bounding box / bounding sphere from the M2 header | MD20 header collisionSphere and boundSphere (a min/max/radius pair each), plus the collision mesh in nBoundingVertices / nBoundingTriangles / nBoundingNormals | wow/modelheaders.h:100-101 (the two spheres), :103-108 (the collision mesh); collision mesh read at wow/WoWModel.cpp:612-626 | The spheres are never used for anything but an XML dump (wow/WoWModel.cpp:3617-3622). The collision mesh is drawn only as a wireframe debug volume: wow/WoWModel.cpp:2532-2545, called from :2457 | none -- Scripts/Wow/M2Parser.cs never seeks past 0x90, so the sphere and collision fields are not read at all | none | out of scope | Nothing visible. Neither renderer uses the header's bounds to draw or to frame the model; both measure their own box from the vertices. The collision mesh is a debug overlay in legacy and simply absent in Unity. | none |
| Bounds used for framing, and what they cover | derived from vertex positions, not stored | app/OrbitCamera.cpp:50-60 -- component-wise min/max over EVERY entry of m->origVertices | app/OrbitCamera.cpp:61-63 (centre + sphere radius) -> frameBounds at :71-82; invoked on model load at app/modelcanvas.cpp:377 | Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:999-1017 (BoundsOfAll) | Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:679 (mesh.bounds), :723 (smr.localBounds), :797 (result.Bounds); consumed at Scripts/WmvMain.cs:438 | partial | The two measure different sets of vertices. Legacy takes the AABB of ALL model vertices, whatever is drawn. Unity takes the AABB only of positions referenced by triangle sets that were actually built (BoundsOfAll iterates triangleSets, WmvModelBuilder.cs:1003-1005) -- and a batch hidden at rest never reaches triangleSets, because the loop skips it at WmvModelBuilder.cs:586 before the Add at :592. So on a model with a large hidden-at-rest batch, Unity's box is smaller than legacy's and the auto-framed camera sits closer. Both deliberately include geoset-hidden submeshes (WmvModelBuilder.cs:677-679) so a variant change does not make the camera jump. | E&#8209;attachments&#8209;events&#8209;cameras |
| Default camera angle when a model is framed | n/a | none | app/OrbitCamera.cpp:9-11 and :73-75 -- yaw 0, pitch 90, up = +Z (:39); with updatePosition at :127-132 that puts the camera at target + radius on +X, level with the model's centre. +X is the direction the model faces, so this is a straight-on FRONT elevation. Distance = radius / sin(fov/2) * 1.15 (:79-80) | none | Scripts/WmvOrbitCamera.cs:71-72 -- yaw 30, pitch 15; distance = extents.magnitude / sin(fov/2) * 1.25 (:56-62); applied at :152-156 | partial | A newly loaded model is presented differently. The OpenGL viewport shows it square-on from the front at eye level; Unity shows it from a three-quarter angle 15 degrees above. Unity is also framed slightly looser (1.25 margin against 1.15) and measures its radius as the full box diagonal (extents.magnitude) rather than the distance from centre to the max corner -- in practice the same quantity, so the margin is the only real size difference. | E&#8209;attachments&#8209;events&#8209;cameras |
| Triangle indices and the two-level indirection | SKIN triangles array at 0x0C (uint16 indices into the skin's own vertex lookup) and vertex lookup at 0x04 (uint16 indices into the M2 vertex array) | wow/WoWModel.cpp:1561-1571 -- resolves both hops once at load into a uint32 vector (rawIndices[i] = indexLookup[triangles[i]]) | wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:755-757 (and the identical loops at :703-705 and :778-780) -- walks model->indices from geoset->istart for icount entries | Scripts/Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:52-69 (both arrays, each validated) and :133-147 (BuildTriangles resolves both hops per submesh) | Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:590 (BuildTriangles), :592 (triangleSets), :675 (mesh.SetTriangles) | supported | None, apart from the 64K IndexStart issue on its own row. Both resolve the same two hops in the same order; Unity additionally range-checks every hop and throws a named error instead of over-reading (M2SkinParser.cs:64-67, :140-143). Legacy has a latent type mismatch -- ModelRenderPass.cpp:750 passes a uint32 index array to glDrawRangeElements as GL_UNSIGNED_SHORT -- but that branch is dead: Source/games/wow/video.cpp:134-135 forces supportVBO = false, so the immediate-mode loop at :755 is what actually runs. | none |
| Triangle winding and back-face culling | SKIN triangle order (WoW's own winding), plus material flag 0x04 TWOSIDED | wow/WoWModel.cpp:1811 (pass->cull = (rf.flags & RENDERFLAGS_TWOSIDED) == 0) | wow/WoWModel.cpp:2317 (glFrontFace(GL_CCW); GL_CW at :2312 only when the model is mirrored) with the file's winding untouched; per-pass cull toggled at wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:526-529 | Scripts/Wow/M2Model.cs:153 (TwoSided = flags & 0x04) | Scripts/Wow/WowCoordinateConverter.cs:100-110 (FlipWinding), called per submesh at Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:591; cull mode set at Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:1544-1547 | supported | None. Unity reverses each triangle's winding exactly once, to compensate for the determinant -1 axis map, and the reasoning is written down at WowCoordinateConverter.cs:17-20. The two-sided flag maps cleanly: legacy disables GL_CULL_FACE, Unity sets _Cull to Off. A model that appeared inside-out would mean the flip had been applied twice or not at all, and it is applied in exactly one place. | none |
| Coordinate-system conversion | M2 model space: right-handed, Z up, +X forward, +Y to the model's left, 1 unit ~ 1 yard | none needed | no conversion at all -- the OpenGL viewport renders WoW model space directly and orients its camera to suit (up = +Z at app/OrbitCamera.cpp:39 and :73) | Scripts/Wow/WowCoordinateConverter.cs:34-39 (positions), :45-50 (normals), :67-73 (rotations), :79-84 (scales), :90-94 (texcoords) | applied at Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:503-513 (vertices) and :392 (bone pivots) | supported | None. The map is x = -wow.y, y = wow.z, z = wow.x, applied in exactly one file by design (WowCoordinateConverter.cs:3-5), with the winding flip that a determinant -1 map requires. Rotations correctly get the conjugated form rather than the position map (:67-73), which is the one place this is easy to get wrong. Scale is left at 1.0 (:31), so a Unity model is about 9 percent smaller than a metre-true one -- invisible, because both renderers frame by bounds. | none |

Corrections applied after the second reading of this domain:

- **Skin profile file (SFID) -- how many exist and which one is loaded** — was status "supported"; symptom "None: both load profile 0 and neither exposes an LOD switch, so both draw the highest-detail mesh.", is status "partial" (missing for legacy-storage models). Unity has ONE way to obtain a skin profile -- a by-FileDataID request for SFID[0] -- and no name-based fallback, so a model whose .m2 is a bare (unchunked) MD20 with a sibling <name>00.skin never renders at all. Symptom: on a legacy-MPQ client the Unity viewport draws nothing and the overlay reads "model has no skin profile (SFID chunk missing) -- nothing to render", while the OpenGL viewport loads the model normally.. Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvMain.cs:295-299 fails the load outright when model.SkinFileDataIDs.Length == 0, and :301 is the only skin request (ipc.RequestAssetByFileDataID(job.Model.SkinFileDataIDs[0])); the pipeline comment at WmvMain.cs:15 states the same single path. A bare MD20 yields no chunks at all -- Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/Wow/M2Parser.cs:415-416 returns an empty chunk table when the file starts with 'MD20', and :96 only reads SFID inside `if (chunks.Count > 0)` -- so SkinFileDataIDs stays at its empty default (Wow/M2Model.cs:236). Even a populated SFID would not help there: Source/wowmodelviewer/UnityAssetAccess.cpp:363-369 refuses every by-FileDataID request when the active client is not CASC or is name-lookup only. Legacy covers exactly this case: Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1525-1535 builds "<name>%02d.skin" and opens it by name when gamefile->isChunked() is false.
- **Draw batches (texture units) -> one Unity submesh each** — was status "supported"; symptom "None structurally. Both make one draw per batch"; legacy_parse "one ModelRenderPass per unit at Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1653-1655, pushed at :1964", is status "partial". Legacy is NOT one pass per batch unconditionally: on a legacy-MPQ client it deletes the WotLK single-texture env alpha-overlay unit before it can be pushed, so that batch produces no draw at all. Unity builds a triangle set plus a Material for every batch with no equivalent rule, so the reflective overlay legacy suppresses would be drawn as an extra alpha-blended copy of the reflect texture sampled with the body's T1 UVs. Note the divergence is latent rather than observable today, because a legacy-MPQ model never reaches the Unity builder (see the SFID correction) -- but the row's claim about the legacy side is wrong as written.. Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1958-1962: `if (legacyMpqMaterial && pass->textureCount == 1 && pass->useEnvMap && pass->blendmode == 2) { delete pass; continue; }`, with legacyMpqMaterial defined at :1684 as storage == MPQ; that `continue` is the only pass drop between the loop head at :1653 and the push at :1964 (the only other `continue` in the range, :1803, is commented out). Unity's loop at Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:570-667 adds a triangle set (:592) and a Material (:665) for every batch, and its only skip is the rest-visibility test at :585-586.
- **Geoset visibility override for legacy-storage creature models** — was symptom "the Unity renderer shows the stripped base mesh: bald, earless, missing the drum kit or microphone that the OpenGL viewport draws" (status "missing"), is status "missing" is right, but the symptom is not reachable as described in this tree: a legacy-MPQ model never gets as far as geoset selection in Unity, because Unity cannot fetch its skin profile at all. The correct symptom is that the model does not render in the Unity viewport at all (load fails), so the override gap is latent behind that. Note also that when the host reports no selection Unity's own default is to draw EVERY submesh (WmvModelBuilder.cs:301-302), which would coincide with the legacy override rather than diverge from it -- the bald/earless outcome only follows when the host does report a set that omits those ids.. Source/wowmodelviewer/UnityAssetAccess.cpp:363-369 refuses by-FileDataID lookups for a name-lookup (MPQ) client, and Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvMain.cs:295-301 has no other way to request a .skin; the override itself is at Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1638-1643, gated on storage == MPQ. The all-visible default is Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:299-303.
- **Bounds used for framing, and what they cover** — was "measures its radius as the full box diagonal (extents.magnitude) rather than the distance from centre to the max corner -- in practice the same quantity", is Not "in practice" -- identically the same quantity by definition. Bounds.extents is the HALF-size, so extents.magnitude IS the centre-to-max-corner distance, which is exactly what legacy computes; neither measures a full diagonal. The rest of the row (status "partial", the hidden-batch bounds shrink, the 1.25 vs 1.15 margin) is correct.. Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvOrbitCamera.cs:56 `float radius = bounds.extents.magnitude;` against Source/wowmodelviewer/OrbitCamera.cpp:62-63 `const glm::vec3 d = mx - center; frameBounds(center, sqrtf(d.x*d.x + d.y*d.y + d.z*d.z));`.

Named by the second reading as not yet covered here: Billboarded bones -- geometry re-oriented to face the viewer. Legacy reads the bone flag (Source/games/wow/Bone.cpp:99, MODELBONE_BILLBOARD) and overwrites the bone matrix's axes from the modelview matrix at Source/games/wow/Bone.cpp:39-54, and because `billboard` forces the transform branch on at Bone.cpp:25 this happens even in the rest pose; WoWModel.cpp:345-349 also forces per-instance animation for such a model. Unity parses the same flag at Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/Wow/M2Model.cs:122-125 and NOTHING consumes it (that property and the material-flag one at :157 are the only occurrences of "Billboard" under Assets/Scripts), so billboarded geometry -- effect planes, flares, some glow cards -- faces a fixed direction in Unity and the camera in the OpenGL viewport., Batch materialLayer / the texture-unit lookup table (legacy ModelTexUnit::texunit, modelheaders.h:300). The report's draw-batch row enumerates the 24-byte record field by field and calls the mapping byte-for-byte faithful, but never says what happens to this word. Unity parses it as MaterialLayer at Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:113 and never reads it again; legacy uses the same word as an index into texunitlookup[] at Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1816 (env-map decision) and, for legacy-MPQ materials, as the source of per-unit UV routing at WoWModel.cpp:1689-1710 -- the table Unity has no access to at all..

### Textures and colour resolution

This domain covers how a BLP file becomes texels on the model, and how a texture slot the M2 does not name becomes a concrete file. On the decoding side the Unity renderer is already at or ahead of the legacy viewport: it handles palettized (alpha 0/1/4/8), DXT1/3/5 and raw BGRA, bounds-checks every read, and names anything it cannot decode, whereas the legacy decoder cannot read raw-BGRA BLPs at all, has a small 4-bit-alpha scaling bug, and picks the DXT sub-format from the alpha depth rather than from the header field that names the format (the two rules disagree on three real header combinations, and a wrong pick means a 8-vs-16-byte block stride and total garbage). The headline gap is not decoding, it is RESOLUTION: Unity only ever fetches a texture by FileDataID, so any M2 that names its textures by an embedded filename string instead of a TXID chunk — every pre-chunked MPQ-era model — renders completely untextured even though the parser already reads the name and the host already exposes a by-path fetch. Of the WoW texture types, only the creature-skin types 11/12/13 are wired end to end (database, current viewport selection, and a listing-based convention fallback all reach Unity correctly by type rather than by position); types 1, 2, 3, 6-8, 10 and 19 — the whole character and equipment set, including the layered body and eye composites the legacy side builds — have no Unity path at all. Three smaller but visible divergences round it out: the M2 per-texture wrap/clamp flags are parsed on the Unity side and then read by nothing, the M2 colour track tints the texture in legacy but is used only as a visibility gate in Unity, and the texture animation matrix is absent so scrolling glow layers stand still. Finally, colour space is unverifiable from this tree: legacy is unambiguously gamma-space (plain GL_RGBA8, no sRGB framebuffer anywhere), while Unity uploads textures flagged sRGB and the project's Color Space setting is not committed and is not mentioned in the build instructions.

Corrections applied after the second reading of this domain:

- **Texture type 2 — object skin / cape** — was status "missing"; "A cape or an item-component model shows untextured in the Unity viewport"; notes conclude "the Unity side has no item/equipment pipeline to hang it on". legacy_render also cites Source/wowmodelviewer/app.cpp:1174., is status "partial". A standalone-displayed item/cape model DOES get its type-2 texture in Unity — the whole chain exists and is type-generic, no item-specific pipeline is needed. The real gap is only equipment worn on a character (the WoWItem path). The app.cpp citation is also wrong.. The skin selector puts item groups in the list with grp.base = TEXTURE_OBJECT_SKIN (Source/wowmodelviewer/animcontrol.cpp:839, 889, 958, 983, 1010) and those groups reach skinList (animcontrol.cpp:1143 iterates skins, 1150 skinList->Append). AnimControl::selectedSkinTextures emits byType[grp->base + (int)i] = fdid (animcontrol.cpp:1522). The host forwards that type verbatim: Source/wowmodelviewer/UnityAssetAccess.cpp:262 `t.type = selected[i].first;` (path 0, gated on the displayed model at UnityAssetAccess.cpp:252-253), serialised at Source/wowmodelviewer/UnityIpcServer.cpp:287 `o["type"] = t.type;`. On the Unity side SlotsForTexture matches ANY declared type, not just 11/12/13: `if ((int)model.Textures[i].Type == t.type) slots.Add(i);` (Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvMain.cs:1277-1281), and the slot is then fetched, decoded and bound like any other (WmvMain.cs:364-366, WmvModelBuilder.cs:1521 `m.mainTexture = tex`). Separately, Source/wowmodelviewer/app.cpp:1174 is the closing brace of the "-build" command-line branch; the actual type-2 write in app.cpp is at app.cpp:1287 `m->updateTextureList(skin, TEXTURE_OBJECT_SKIN);`.
- **Palettized 4-bit alpha plane** — was status "supported"; symptom "fully opaque texels come out 240 instead of 255 ... a faint uniform translucency"; notes "for the odd pixel it masks 0xF0 and multiplies WITHOUT shifting the nibble down"., is status "partial". The mask for the odd pixel is 0x1E, not 0xF0, so the odd texel's alpha is read from bits 1-4 of the byte — a mix of the wrong nibble's high bits and the right nibble's low bit. The result is arbitrary per byte, not a uniform darkening, and the ceiling is 254, not 240.. Source/games/wow/Texture.cpp:248 `alpha = (*a & (0xf << cnt++)) * 0x11;` with cnt taking only values 0 and 1 (reset at Texture.cpp:249-253), so the odd-pixel mask is 0xf<<1 = 0x1E. Source/games/wow/Texture.cpp:268 `k \|= alpha << 24;` keeps only the low 8 bits of the product. Worked cases: byte 0xFF -> odd texel (0xFF & 0x1E)*0x11 = 510 -> 254; byte 0xF0 (odd nibble = 15, fully opaque) -> (0x10)*0x11 = 272 -> 16, i.e. almost fully transparent; byte 0x0F (odd nibble = 0, fully transparent) -> (0x0E)*0x11 = 238. Unity is exact and correct: Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/Wow/BlpDecoder.cs:156-158 `int nibble = ((i & 1) == 0) ? (both & 0x0F) : (both >> 4); a = (byte)(nibble * 17);`.
- **Frame-buffer colour space / project colour space** — was notes: "the GL canvas is requested with only WX_GL_RGBA / WX_GL_DOUBLEBUFFER / WX_GL_DEPTH_SIZE 24 (modelcanvas.cpp:72)"., is Status "unknown" is right, but the legacy half of the citation does not hold for this Windows tree: that attribute list is compiled out on Windows, so the shipped build takes wxWidgets' default pixel format, which this tree does not pin either. The row's conclusion (no sRGB encode anywhere in the legacy pipeline) survives on other evidence.. Source/wowmodelviewer/modelcanvas.cpp:70 `#ifndef _WINDOWS`, :71 `namespace {`, :72 the attrib[] array, :73 `}`, :74 `#endif`; the only consumer is the base-class initialiser Source/wowmodelviewer/modelcanvas.cpp:127, itself guarded by `#ifndef _WINDOWS` at modelcanvas.cpp:126 / `#endif` at :128. The surviving support for "legacy is gamma-space": the only sRGB mentions anywhere under Source/ are Source/plugins/exporters/fbx/FBXExporter.cpp:1353 and :1356, Source/wowmodelviewer/ImageSequenceExporter.cpp:164 and Source/wowmodelviewer/util.cpp:179 — none in a GL call — and every upload is a plain internal format (Source/games/wow/Texture.cpp:93, :164, :169, :273).
- **Texture units bound per batch** — was notes: "binds tex2/tex3/tex4 at WoWModel.cpp:1796-1798" and "Legacy clamps to 4 units (WoWModel.cpp:1658)"., is The extra units are bound at Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1791-1793, and the clamp is at WoWModel.cpp:1659. WoWModel.cpp:1796 is the single-unit bind `pass->tex = texLk(...)`. Status "partial" and every Unity-side citation in this row are correct.. Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1786 `if (shaderTexCount > 1)`, :1790 `texOffset = 0;`, :1791 `pass->tex2 = texLk(Tex[j].textureid + 1);`, :1792 tex3, :1793 tex4, :1796 `pass->tex = texLk(Tex[j].textureid + texOffset);`. WoWModel.cpp:1658 is `uint texCount = Tex[j].op_count;`, the clamp is WoWModel.cpp:1659 `if (texCount > 4) texCount = 4;`. Unity side verified as claimed: two samplers at Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Resources/WmvOpaque.shader:130 and :132, and only units 0 and 1 resolved at WmvModelBuilder.cs:612-613.
- **DXT sub-format selection (which of DXT1/3/5 a DXT-encoded BLP actually is)** — was symptom: "On the combinations where the two rules disagree the block size differs (8 vs 16 bytes) ... one renderer draws diagonal smeared noise"; three divergent combinations listed., is Status "partial" stands, but the symptom applies to only two of the three listed cases. For (alphaSize 4, alphaEncoding 7) legacy picks DXT3 and Unity DXT5 — both 16-byte blocks, so the colour image is identical and only the alpha channel decodes differently. A fourth divergence is also unlisted: alphaSize 8 with preferredFormat other than 0/1/7 gives legacy DXT3 vs Unity DXT5.. Block stride is chosen from the name on both sides: Source/games/wow/Texture.cpp:123 `int blocksize = 8;` raised to 16 only at Texture.cpp:130 and :143; Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/Wow/BlpDecoder.cs:195 `int blockBytes = (kind == "dxt1") ? 8 : 16;`. Legacy's rule: Texture.cpp:127-131 (alphaSize 8 or 4 -> DXT3), :133-136, :140-144 (DXT5 only when attr[1]==8 && attr[2]==7). Unity's rule: BlpDecoder.cs:116-118. For alphaSize 8 / preferredFormat 2, legacy falls through 140 and stays DXT3 while BlpDecoder.cs:118 returns dxt5.
- **GPU-compressed texture upload vs CPU decode to RGBA32** — was symptom: "Unity uses roughly 6x the texture memory per skin (a 512x512 DXT5 skin is 256 KB compressed, 1 MB as RGBA32)"., is 4x for DXT5/DXT3 (which is what the row's own 256 KB -> 1 MB example computes) and 8x for DXT1. Both sides upload a mip chain, so the pyramid overhead does not change the ratio. Status "partial" and the rest of the row are correct.. Unity always creates RGBA32 (Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:1488 `new Texture2D(img.Width, img.Height, TextureFormat.RGBA32, true)`) = 4 bytes/texel; legacy keeps the 16-byte-per-4x4-block DXT3/DXT5 payload (1 byte/texel) or the 8-byte DXT1 block (0.5 byte/texel) on the GPU (Source/games/wow/Texture.cpp:130, :143, :123, uploaded at Texture.cpp:164). Both build mips: Texture.cpp:151-179 uploads every stored level, WmvModelBuilder.cs:1490 `tex.Apply(true, false)` generates them.
- **Alpha channel used as a combiner mask** — was legacy_render "Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:650 (uPixelShader uniform into the GLSL combiner)"., is Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:653. Line 650 is the unit-1 sampler binding. Status "supported" is correct.. Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:649 `if (g_uTex0 >= 0) glUniform1i(g_uTex0, 0);`, :650 g_uTex1, :651 g_uTex2, :652 g_uTex3, :653 `if (g_uPixelShader >= 0) glUniform1i(g_uPixelShader, pixelShader);` (the 649-652 range the same row's notes cite for "four samplers" is exactly right).
- **Alpha-test threshold for the alpha-key blend mode** — was legacy_render "Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:493 (glAlphaFunc GL_GEQUAL 0.5)"; notes assert without citation that "the legacy combiner keys there rather than at a rounded half"., is The fixed-path glAlphaFunc is at Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:491 (493 is the `break;`), and the combiner claim is proved by ModelRenderPass.cpp:655, which passes exactly 0.501960814f — i.e. 128/255, matching Unity. Status "supported" is correct.. Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:486 `case BM_TRANSPARENT:`, :487 `glEnable(GL_ALPHA_TEST);`, :491 `glAlphaFunc(GL_GEQUAL, 0.5f);`, :492 `glBlendFunc(GL_ONE, GL_ZERO);`, :493 `break;`. Combiner threshold: ModelRenderPass.cpp:655 `if (g_uAlphaTest >= 0) glUniform1f(g_uAlphaTest, 0.501960814f);`. Unity: WmvModelBuilder.cs:1541 `ApplyBlendMode(m, mode, def.DepthWriteDisabled, 128f / 255f, out treatedAs);`.
- **texUnitLookup table (pre-shaderId UV routing, MPQ-era models)** — was notes: "Legacy only consults the table when the active client storage is MPQ (WoWModel.cpp:1682, 1685)"., is Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1684 (the storage test) and WoWModel.cpp:1689 (the guard around the table read). 1682 is a comment line and 1685 is `uint shaderTexCount = texCount;`. Status "missing" and every other citation in this row check out.. Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1684 `const bool legacyMpqMaterial = (GAMEDIRECTORY.clientProfile().storage == core::StorageType::MPQ);`, :1689 `if (legacyMpqMaterial)`, :1691 `const int tu = (int)Tex[j].texunit;`, :1693-1701 the tulUv lambda (cited correctly). Unity really has no such offset: the header offsets it reads are Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/Wow/M2Parser.cs:55-67 and there is no OfsTexUnitLookup anywhere under Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets.
- **Preferring the creature-skin unit within a degraded multi-texture batch** — was notes: "WoWModel.cpp:1795 resets texOffset for real multi-texture batches"; legacy_render cited as WoWModel.cpp:1800., is The reset is at Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1790 (1795 is blank) and the bind that consumes texOffset is at WoWModel.cpp:1796 (1800 is blank). Status "partial" and the reasoning are correct.. Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1786 `if (shaderTexCount > 1)` ... :1790 `texOffset = 0;` ... :1794 `}` ... :1796 `pass->tex = texLk(Tex[j].textureid + texOffset);`, :1805 `pass->color = Tex[j].colorIndex;`. The scan that sets texOffset is WoWModel.cpp:1734-1743 (notes say 1735-1743) and the degraded-multi-texture collapse at WoWModel.cpp:1714-1715 is cited exactly right.
- **Texture type 1 — character body/clothes composite** — was notes: "Unity's host-side resolver never emits type 1 (Source/wowmodelviewer/UnityAssetAccess.cpp:301 only emits TEXTURE_GAMEOBJECT1+i)"., is Status "missing" stands, but the stated reason is not the whole resolver: it has a second emitter that forwards ANY type. Type 1 never appears because no skin group in the app ever sets grp.base = TEXTURE_SKIN, not because the resolver is hard-wired to 11/12/13.. Source/wowmodelviewer/UnityAssetAccess.cpp:262 `t.type = selected[i].first;` — the selection path, tried before the database (UnityAssetAccess.cpp:252-268) — carries whatever base the selected TextureGroup holds. Every grp.base assignment in Source/wowmodelviewer/animcontrol.cpp is either TEXTURE_GAMEOBJECT1 (lines 590, 694, 734) or TEXTURE_OBJECT_SKIN (lines 839, 889, 958, 983, 1010); none is TEXTURE_SKIN (wow_enums.h:305). The rest of the row is confirmed: CharTexture::compose at Source/games/wow/CharTexture.cpp:45, the bind at Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:3496, and no compositor under Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts.
- **Texture lookup table (textureCombos / texlookup) — and the other rows citing this region** — was legacy_parse WoWModel.cpp:1725, legacy_render WoWModel.cpp:1800, notes "the texLk lambda at WoWModel.cpp:1726"; the sibling rows cite WoWModel.cpp:3519 for getGLTexture and WoWModel.cpp:2669 for updateTextureList's break., is The texLk lambda is Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1727, the bind is WoWModel.cpp:1796, WoWModel::getGLTexture is WoWModel.cpp:3521, and the break in updateTextureList is WoWModel.cpp:2668. Every status in these rows ("supported", "supported", "supported") is correct; only the anchors in this file drift by 1-5 lines.. Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1726 `const uint nTexLk = (uint)header.nTexLookup;`, :1727 `auto texLk = [&](uint idx) -> uint16 { return (idx < nTexLk) ? texlookup[idx] : (uint16)0; };`, :1796 `pass->tex = texLk(...)`; :2667 `replaceTextures[special] = newId;` (cited correctly elsewhere), :2668 `break;`; :3518 `return TEXTUREMANAGER.get(getGLTexture(Tex));`, :3519 `}`, :3521 `GLuint WoWModel::getGLTexture(uint16 Tex) const`. Unity-side anchors in these rows are exact (WmvModelBuilder.cs:1336-1349, M2Parser.cs:202, M2Parser.cs:186/188).

Named by the second reading as not yet covered here: Per-batch texture-COORDINATE routing (M2 skin batch textureCoordComboIndex). Unity parses it and nothing reads it: Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:116 `b.TextureCoordComboIndex = c.ReadUInt16();` into M2Model.cs:344, with no other occurrence anywhere under Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts. Unit 1's UV set is instead derived purely from the shaderId table (WmvModelBuilder.cs:605 `M2UvSource unit1Uv = ... shader.UvSource[1]`, selected in the shader at Assets/Resources/WmvOpaque.shader:345). The report's "Second stored UV set" row covers the vertex attribute but never this indirection., No de-duplication of a texture across SLOTS on the Unity side, at either the fetch or the decode stage. RequestTextures issues one IPC request per slot (WmvMain.cs:337-339) and HandleModelTextures one per (texture, slot) pair (WmvMain.cs:362-369), and OnTextureBytes decodes each into its own BlpImage keyed by slot (WmvMain.cs:385-386), so a file two slots share is transferred and CPU-decoded twice. Legacy dedups by file name at TextureManager::add (Source/games/wow/TextureManager.cpp:24-27) before any decode happens. The "Texture de-duplication and lifetime" row only compares the Texture2D upload cache (WmvModelBuilder.cs:1427)., The legacy character composite is uploaded into GL texture object 0. Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:3492 `const GLuint charTex = 0;` then :3493 `tex.compose(charTex);` and :3496 `replaceTextures[TEXTURE_SKIN] = charTex;`, and CharTexture::compose binds and uploads to exactly that id (Source/games/wow/CharTexture.cpp:68-69). So every character's body composite lives on the shared default texture object rather than a name from TextureManager. The "Texture type 1" row asserts the bind but not that the target is id 0., Magnification filter. The report covers minification only. Legacy sets GL_TEXTURE_MAG_FILTER = GL_LINEAR at Source/games/wow/Texture.cpp:302 (and again for the char composite at Source/games/wow/CharTexture.cpp:71); Unity sets FilterMode.Bilinear at Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:1492, which is the same thing on magnification. It is parity, but it is the other half of the sampler state the "Mip sampling / minification filter" row measures..

### Materials and render state

Materials and render state is the domain that turns one M2 draw batch — a 16-bit render-flag word, a 16-bit blend mode, a 16-bit shader id and a texture count — into blend function, depth state, face culling, alpha test and a texture combiner. Both renderers read the SAME two fields (ModelRenderFlags.flags/blend via WoWModel.cpp:1799-1830, and M2MaterialDef.Flags/BlendMode via M2Parser.cs:191-200) and both carry a name-for-name identical 36-entry shader table (WoWModel.cpp:1315-1351 vs M2ShaderTable.cs:38-73), so the classification layer is genuinely at parity. The blend functions for all eight modes are also exactly equal, and depth write is taken from the model's own flag on both sides. The headline findings are three: (1) the alpha-key cutout is BROKEN in Unity for most materials — the shader's clip() tests an alpha that WmvModelBuilder.cs:1534 forces to 1.0 whenever the resolved combiner's AlphaMode is not exactly 1 or unit 1 is not bound, so a blend-mode-1 batch whose pixel shader is Combiners_Opaque (shaderId 0, the single commonest case) renders as a solid quad instead of a cutout; (2) the UNLIT flag (0x01) is parsed in Unity (M2Model.cs:151) and never read anywhere, while Unity instead invents fullbright from the blend mode alone (WmvOpaque.shader:538-542) — so self-illuminated parts are dimmed and additive parts are brightened relative to the legacy viewport; (3) Unity ignores uvSource[0], so a material whose FIRST unit is an environment sphere map samples it with the mesh's stored UVs, and it draws submeshes in raw skin-batch order with no equivalent of the legacy blend-mode sort (WoWModel.cpp:1968/2011-2018). Combiner coverage is otherwise honest: 15 of 37 pixel-shader ids are reproduced exactly, 9 more collapse identically to legacy because legacy also drops the specular lobe by default, and 13 fall to a logged unit-0-only fallback.

Corrections applied after the second reading of this domain:

- **Draw order / sorting of passes** — was partial; the legacy comparator is described as "blend mode ascending, then geoset index, then special texture", with the claims that legacy "guarantees alpha-blend (2) before additive (3,4) before modulate (5,6) before premultiplied (7)" and that "Assigning renderQueue = 3000 + blendMode would reproduce the legacy comparator's primary key exactly", is partial (status itself is right), but the key precedence is inverted: specialTex is the FIRST key tested, before blend mode. Two passes that share a geoIndex are ordered by specialTex regardless of blend mode, so legacy guarantees NO blend-mode ordering for exactly the overlapping-layer case the symptom describes, and 3000+blendMode does not reproduce the comparator's primary key. (The comparator is also not a strict weak ordering, so the sorted result for a mixed set is not well-defined.). Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:2011-2018 — line 2013-2014 `if (mrp1->geoIndex == mrp2->geoIndex) return mrp1->specialTex < mrp2->specialTex;` is evaluated BEFORE line 2015-2016 `if (mrp1->blendmode == mrp2->blendmode) return (mrp1->geoIndex < mrp2->geoIndex);` and line 2017 `return mrp1->blendmode < mrp2->blendmode;`. Applied at Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1968, drawn at Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:2390.
- **Alpha-key cutout: WHICH alpha the test compares** — was partial; notes say "Only ids 1, 9, 10, 11, 16, 23, 33 and the unknown-shader fallback (line 1188, AlphaMode 1) currently cut out" and "The minimal fix is to force _AlphaMode >= 1 whenever the blend mode is AlphaKey, mirroring the fact that legacy's alpha test lives OUTSIDE its combiner", is partial (the defect is real and confirmed — status unchanged), but the scope and the prescribed fix are wrong. (a) ids 2, 3, 6, 7, 8, 21 and 36 also cut out whenever unit 1 is bound. (b) Legacy's alpha test does NOT live outside its combiner on the multi-texture path: the discard is inside the GLSL and gated on can_discard, which is never set for ps 0/4/5/12/13/14/15/18/19/20/22/24/25/27/29/32 — so legacy also draws those multi-texture alpha-key quads uncut, Unity already matches there, and forcing _AlphaMode>=1 would introduce a NEW divergence. The genuine divergence is confined to the SINGLE-texture path, which always falls to fixed-function GL_MODULATE.. Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:1163-1175 assigns AlphaMode 4 (ps8, ps21), 2 (ps2, ps3), 3 (ps6, ps7, ps36), 1 (ps9, ps11); Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Resources/WmvOpaque.shader:368-371 turns AlphaMode 2/3/4 into a real alpha before the clip at :375. Legacy's discard is inside the combiner at Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:148 (`if (can_discard && discard_alpha < u_alpha_test) do_discard = true;`) and can_discard is never set at Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:107 (ps0), :111 (ps4), :112 (ps5), :119 (ps12), :120 (ps13), :121 (ps14), :122 (ps15), :127 (ps20), :129 (ps22), :136 (ps29). Single-texture batches never reach that path: Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1875-1886 sets M2SV_COMBINER only when shaderTexCount > 1.
- **Combiner arithmetic: specular-lobe collapses (pixel shaders 8, 10, 13, 14, 16, 20, 21, 23, 33)** — was supported; ps33 is grouped with the combiners Unity matches only because legacy's specular lobe is weighted 0 by default, and is counted in the summary's "9 more collapse identically to legacy because legacy also drops the specular lobe", is supported (status unchanged), but ps33 does not belong in this group — legacy's ps33 has no specular term at all, so Unity reproduces it exactly rather than by collapse. The summary's split is 16 reproduced exactly / 8 collapsed, not 15 / 9.. Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:140 — `else if (ps == 33) { mat_diffuse = mesh_color * tex1.rgb; discard_alpha = tex1.a; can_discard = true; }`, with no `specular =` assignment, unlike ps8 (:115), ps10 (:117), ps13 (:120), ps14 (:121), ps16 (:123), ps20 (:127), ps21 (:128), ps23 (:130). Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:1160 annotates it `// Mod_Depth` without the `(lobe dropped)` marker the real collapses carry at :1154-1159.
- **Render flag bit 0x08 - BILLBOARD** — was partial; note claims M2Model.cs "documents the bit as 'deliberately unused', which is accurate for the transform but understates it: legacy DOES consume the bit, as one third of the useEnvMap predicate", is missing — Unity has zero read sites for the bit and reproduces none of its only legacy effect, which is the same evidence the report used to call the UNLIT row "missing". And the note is factually wrong: the cited Unity comment already states legacy's env-mapping use verbatim, so it understates nothing.. Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/Wow/M2Model.cs:154-156 reads "Bit 0x08 is BILLBOARD in the legacy viewport, not a depth-test flag: it only feeds that renderer's environment-mapping decision, and nothing there ever disables the depth test." A grep for `.Billboard` across Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets returns only the two property declarations (Wow/M2Model.cs:125 and Wow/M2Model.cs:157) and no read site — identical to the `.Unlit` result at Wow/M2Model.cs:151 that the report grades "missing".
- **Texture units 3 and 4 (op_count > 2)** — was missing; note calls the missing samplers "the blocking prerequisite for pixel shaders 15, 19, 25, 27, 30, 32, 35 and the guild family", is missing (status unchanged), but ps15 and ps31 are not blocked on extra samplers and should be dropped from that list: ps15's third unit feeds only the default-zero specular lobe (which the report's own adjacent row calls "EXACT against legacy's default"), and ps31 (Guild_NoBorder, part of "the guild family") reads only two units.. Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:122 — ps15's tex3 appears only in `specular = tex3.rgb * tex3.a * u_tex_sample_alpha.b`, and that lobe is multiplied by u_specular_weight at Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:156, uploaded as 0 by default at Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:662. Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:138 — ps31 is `mat_diffuse = mesh_color * tex1.rgb * mix(g0, tex2.rgb, vec3(tex2.a)); discard_alpha = tex1.a; can_discard = true;` — tex1 and tex2 only.
- **Combiner arithmetic: unimplemented combiners (17, 18, 19, 24, 25, 26, 27, 28, 30, 31, 32, 34, 35)** — was partial; symptom says these "render flat: only their first texture appears" and lists "the Illum self-lit form (34)" among the materials that "lose their extra layers", is partial (status unchanged), but ps34 diverges in the opposite direction: legacy draws it BLACK, because its combiner arm assigns no mat_diffuse and the variable keeps its vec3(0.0) initialisation. Unity's fallback draws unit 0's colour — more than legacy, not less — so ps34 is not an example of Unity losing a layer.. Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:141 — `else if (ps == 34) { discard_alpha = tex1.a; can_discard = true; }`, with `vec3 mat_diffuse = vec3(0.0);` at Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:101 and the unmodified value emitted at Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:156. Unity's default arm is `Plan(0, 1, 1f, false)` at Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:1188, which draws unit 0 via Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Resources/WmvOpaque.shader:351.

Named by the second reading as not yet covered here: Texture minification/magnification filter and mipmapping. Legacy uploads every mip level but sets only GL_LINEAR for both filters — the LINEAR_MIPMAP_LINEAR branch is inside a comment block (Source/games/wow/Texture.cpp:295-302), and no other site sets GL_TEXTURE_MIN_FILTER for M2 textures — so no mip chain is ever sampled. Unity builds one and uses it: Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:1488-1492 (`new Texture2D(img.Width, img.Height, TextureFormat.RGBA32, true)`, `tex.Apply(true, false)`, `tex.filterMode = FilterMode.Bilinear`). The report gives the sibling wrap state its own row (Texture wrap flags) but never covers filter state, so a real minification difference between the two pictures is unaudited., Texture transform / UV animation has no row of its own, only a passing mention inside the 'Batch flags byte' note. Legacy resolves a per-unit texanim and texanim2 (Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1848-1866) and pushes a GL texture matrix for unit 0 (Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:560-567) and for unit 1 (Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:630-636). Unity parses TextureTransformComboIndex (Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:118), never reads it, and WmvOpaque.shader has no texture-matrix uniform (its only UV expressions are :314 TRANSFORM_TEX, :342 and :345). Given the report dedicates rows to the colour and transparency tracks under stage B-animated-material, this belongs as its own row., The audit checks the README's claims (row 44) but not the equivalent claim made in code. Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvShadowRig.cs:26-30 states that 'alpha-keyed batches (hair cards, fur flaps) clip in the shadow pass exactly as they clip on screen, so they cast shaped shadows rather than solid slabs' — falsified by the same _AlphaMode defect the report documents (Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Scripts/WmvModelBuilder.cs:1534 plus Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Resources/WmvOpaque.shader:367-375), and the report's own alpha-key symptom says the quad 'casts a slab-shaped shadow'..

### Animated surface effects

Animated surface effects — UV transforms, colour tracks and transparency tracks — are the domain where the Unity renderer's parse/apply split is widest, and the split runs in an unusual direction: three of the four inputs are parsed but only as a single frozen sample, while the fourth is not parsed at all. The texture_transforms array (MD20 header 0x60) and its lookup table (0x98) are entirely absent from the Unity parser — there is no constant for either offset in M2Parser.cs:57-67 — so UV scrolling, rotation and scaling do not exist in any form; only the per-batch TextureTransformComboIndex is read (M2SkinParser.cs:118) and nothing consumes it. The colors[] and texture_weights[] arrays ARE parsed, but reduced to one boolean and two floats at animation 0, time 0 (M2Parser.cs:322-348), and those values are used solely as a build-time show/hide gate on the batch (WmvModelBuilder.cs:1371-1409) — the RGB numbers are never even read, and no value ever reaches a material. Global sequences are parsed and correctly timed, but only for bone tracks (WmvM2Animator.cs:405-413); ReadTrackFirstValue deliberately skips the globalSequence and interpolation fields (M2Parser.cs:389) and never reads the timestamps, so there is nothing to interpolate and no clock driving a material — no MonoBehaviour under Assets/Scripts touches a Material per frame. The legacy renderer, by contrast, re-evaluates all of this every frame (ModelRenderPass::init at WoWModel.cpp:2392, texAnims calc at WoWModel.cpp:2301-2302) off two clocks. The visible consequence is that every pulsing, scrolling, blinking or tinted surface in Unity is a still image locked to whatever it happened to be at load, and geometry that should appear later in a cycle is deleted outright; the README's "not yet implemented" claim (docs/unity-renderer/README.md:650-654) is accurate.

Named by the second reading as not yet covered here: TEXTUREUNIT_IGNORE_TEXTURE_WEIGHTS (batch flag bit 0x40), defined at Source/games/wow/modelheaders.h:288. The report covers the 0x10 static bit but never mentions this one. It is dead in BOTH renderers - grep over Source/games/wow finds the #define and no consumer, and the Unity tree never reads batch.Flags at all (the only '.Flags' read in WmvModelBuilder.cs is mat.Flags at WmvModelBuilder.cs:640, with the batch byte parsed and dropped at Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:106 -> Wow/M2Model.cs:330). It matters for this domain because a batch that sets it should IGNORE the texture-weight track that Unity's build-time gate (WmvModelBuilder.cs:1376-1390) currently uses to delete the batch outright., Animated alpha promoting an opaque/alpha-key batch to blended. Legacy re-decides blend STATE from the animated alpha every frame: Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:577 'if (blendmode<=1 && ocol.w<1.0f) glEnable(GL_BLEND);', where ocol.w is the product of the colour-alpha and texture-weight tracks computed at ModelRenderPass.cpp:392-444. Unity fixes blend state once at build from the M2 blend mode alone (WmvModelBuilder.cs:1541 ApplyBlendMode, called inside the per-batch material build) and has no path that can turn blending on when a track dips below 1, so a batch that legacy fades out will stay hard-edged and fully opaque in Unity even after the tracks are parsed., The legacy renderer already contains a cycle-peak track evaluator, Source/games/wow/ModelRenderPass.cpp:358 'float trackPeakValue(const AnimatedShort & track, ssize_t anim)', used at ModelRenderPass.cpp:403 and :443 to decide the same visibility gate from the track's peak over the whole cycle instead of from one instant. That is precisely the reference implementation for the flaw the report identifies in Unity's build-time gate (evaluated once at t=0, WmvModelBuilder.cs:562 -> BatchIsVisible at :1371), and the report never mentions it exists..

### Bone animation

Bone animation is the one large legacy subsystem the Unity renderer genuinely implements end to end, and for a model whose bones live inside its own .m2 the reproduction is close to exact: the same pivot/parent composition (algebraically identical via the rest-pose = bind-pose construction), the same 16-bit quaternion unpacking, the same "first sequence with AnimId 0" idle rule, the same global-sequence clock that keeps running while the animation is held, external .anim keyframes fetched over the asset channel, and app-driven play/pause/speed/scrub over IPC. The headline finding is structural, not numeric: a model whose bones live in a separate skeleton file (the SKID chunk, and the SKPD parent skeleton it can defer to) gets no rig at all in Unity - M2Parser deliberately refuses the header bone array and WmvModelBuilder falls back to a rigid static mesh - while the legacy viewport reads SKB1 out of that file and animates normally. On modern retail data that is most creatures and every character, so those models simply never move. Below that sit four narrower holes: billboard bones are parsed and never applied; Hermite/Bezier tangents are dropped and the tracks read as linear; fetched .anim bytes are cursored from offset 0 with no chunk scan while the legacy repositions the file to its AFSB chunk; and everything the legacy animation manager does beyond "one sequence, looping forever" - loop counts, the queued animation list, the NextAnimation chain, the secondary upper-body track, the independent mouth track and the closed-fist override - exists in neither the IPC message nor the player. Contrary to docs/unity-renderer/README.md:646, blending between sequences is absent from the legacy renderer too, so that is not a parity gap.
