## Recommended order of work

Each entry is a branch. The order is what the reports imply, not a preference.

**1. `feature/unity-m2-index-integrity`**

- Lands: 24-bit index start (fold the Level word into IndexStart at Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:80 and :138); geoset default when the host reports no selection at all (WmvModelBuilder.cs:301-302 returns true for every submesh, where legacy shows only id 0 -- Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1607 `hdgeo->display = (hdgeo->id == 0);`); a log line when either path fires.
- Why here: First because these are the only defects that silently draw the WRONG triangles rather than too few or too dull ones. Until they are fixed, any visual comparison on a large or multi-variant model may be measuring scrambled or overlapping geometry instead of the feature under test, so every later branch's verification depends on this one. Depends on nothing.
- Scope: Very high -- two parser fields and one predicate; no shader, no host, no new runtime object.

**2. `feature/unity-m2-surface-state (stage A)`**

- Lands: Alpha-key clip fix confined to the single-texture path (WmvModelBuilder.cs:1534 + WmvOpaque.shader:367-375); UNLIT routed into the existing _Emissive property (Wow/M2Model.cs:151 -> WmvModelBuilder.cs:1305); wrap/clamp from the texture Flags word the parser already reads and nothing consumes (Wow/M2Parser.cs:866); uvSource[0] honoured so a unit-0 sphere map is generated rather than sampled with mesh UVs; a deterministic order within the transparent queue, which today is one queue value for six blend modes (WmvModelBuilder.cs:1240-1287) on a single renderer with a single bounds centre (WmvModelBuilder.cs:673, :718).
- Why here: Second because each of these decides the base surface state that every later stage composites on top of: an animated glow or a particle drawn through a wrong emissive, cutout or queue decision looks wrong for a reason that has nothing to do with animation or emitters, and would be debugged twice. Every input is a field the parser already produces, so nothing upstream is required.
- Scope: High -- the material builder in WmvModelBuilder.cs plus WmvOpaque.shader; no new files, no C++ change.

**3. `feature/unity-m2-animated-materials (stage B)`**

- Lands: The 0x60 transform array and its 0x98 lookup; colour and weight read as real tracks through the existing generic reader (Wow/M2Parser.cs:598 ReadTrack<T>, which already handles global sequences, per-sequence entries and external .anim buffers); a per-frame material writer on the animator's clock (WmvM2Animator.cs:271 LateUpdate, GlobalTimeMs at :46 and :280); per-frame visibility replacing the build-time gate (WmvModelBuilder.cs:585-586 and BatchIsVisible at :1371-1409); the STATIC batch bit; the env-unit exemption from the UV matrix; animated alpha promoting an opaque/alpha-key batch to blended.
- Why here: Third: it drives the blend and emissive state that branch 2 fixes, and it reuses two things that already exist rather than inventing them -- the generic track reader and the running clock. It also removes the build-time batch deletion, which is what currently shrinks the framing box (BoundsOfAll iterates only built triangle sets, WmvModelBuilder.cs:999-1005), so the camera-framing divergence closes without a fix of its own. It unblocks branch 4, whose emitters are timed off the same clock.
- Scope: Medium -- parser, builder, one new MonoBehaviour and a unit-1 UV matrix in the shader; all inside Unity.

**4. `feature/unity-m2-effects (stage C)`**

- Lands: Particle emitters, ribbon emitters, and billboard bones (parsed at Wow/M2Model.cs:125, with no read site anywhere under Assets/Scripts).
- Why here: Fourth because emitters have nothing of their own to stand on: they are positioned by bone matrices that already work, timed by the clock branch 3 extends, and drawn with the blend/emissive/queue states branch 2 fixes. Building them earlier means building a second, parallel material and timing path that branches 2 and 3 would then have to be reconciled with.
- Scope: Low -- new parser arrays, a new runtime system and a new draw path. The largest branch in this list; consider splitting ribbons from particles.

**5. `feature/unity-m2-legacy-storage`**

- Lands: By-path skin and texture fetch (the client call exists at WmvIpcClient.cs:385 and the host side at Source/wowmodelviewer/UnityAssetAccess.cpp:164); the name-based <name>NN.skin fallback legacy uses at Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1527-1529; the texUnitLookup UV routing Unity has no access to; and the redundant env alpha-overlay pass legacy deletes at Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1958-1962.
- Why here: Placed here on dependency grounds only -- it is orthogonal to branches 1-4 and improves no model that already renders. It is the one entry whose position is a product call rather than a technical one: if pre-chunked clients matter to this user, it moves to the front, because for those models it is the difference between a blank viewport and a picture.
- Scope: Medium, but two-sided -- the only branch that changes C++ host code as well as C#.

**6. `feature/unity-m2-animation-completeness (stage D)`**

- Lands: SKID/SKPD skeleton fetch (refused today at WmvModelBuilder.cs:335-343); the AFSB chunk offset inside fetched .anim files; Hermite/Bezier tangents (stride is honoured but tangents dropped at Wow/M2Parser.cs:637-641); the .anim filename fallback; and the transport parity the IPC message lacks -- loop counts, the queued list, next-animation chaining, secondary and mouth tracks (Source/games/wow/AnimManager.cpp:49-57, :107-132, :164-179).
- Why here: After the surface stages because bone animation already works for the common case, so each remaining item costs more work per pixel it changes. The exception is SKID, which is total for an affected model but rare by this tree's own measurement (WmvModelBuilder.cs:338-340: one model in a spread of 300) -- if the unknown below overturns that number, this branch moves up. The transport half needs new host IPC fields, which is why it cannot sit inside a Unity-only branch.
- Scope: Medium -- SKID is another chunked format and another asset round trip; the transport work spans the IPC boundary.

**7. `feature/unity-m2-attachments-lights-cameras (stage E)`**

- Lands: Bone-parented attachments (legacy multiplies by the parent bone matrix at Source/games/wow/ModelAttachment.cpp:27 and :33), model lights, model cameras and events.
- Why here: After branch 6 because attachments ride bone matrices that must be correct for every model class first, and before branch 8 because equipment hangs on attachment points. Model lights additionally have to be reconciled with the tuned preview rig -- a decision that should not be forced while earlier branches are still moving the picture.
- Scope: Medium. Split the model-lights part out if the rig decision is unsettled; attachments and cameras are independent of it.

**8. `feature/unity-m2-characters-equipment (stage F)`**

- Lands: The character texture composite (types 1, 2, 6, 7, 8, 10, 19), equipment geosets, and the closed-fist finger override.
- Why here: Last: it needs the by-path/host resolver work from branch 5, attachment points from branch 7, and a compositor that exists nowhere under Assets/Scripts. It is also the only stage whose absence costs nothing at all on the creature models the in-game reference set is made of.
- Scope: Low -- new compositor, new host resolver types, the widest surface across both sides.

## What should not be touched yet

- **The preview light rig constants and the shadow rig's world-anchored key** — KEY_FLOOR 0.265 and KEY_GAIN 1.644 (Tools/UnityRendererProject/Assets/Resources/WmvOpaque.shader:96-97) and the anchoring described at WmvShadowRig.cs:18-24 were tuned by eye and merged as the current HEAD (cb89eefe). The UNLIT work in branch 2 must route the flag into the existing _Emissive property (WmvModelBuilder.cs:1305) and change nothing else; retuning the rig in the same branch makes the emissive change unmeasurable.
- **The `_WmvRig < 0.5` legacy path in WmvOpaque.shader:530-542** — Its own comment states it must stay byte-identical to what WMV drew before the rig work. It is the only A/B baseline inside the renderer; altering it destroys the reference that every later visual comparison is made against.
- **priorityPlane** — It is parsed and unread in Unity (Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:107) and ignored by the legacy renderer too, so implementing it moves the Unity picture AWAY from the only reference the project can currently diff against. Defer until branch 2's within-queue sort exists, then verify against the game rather than against the OpenGL viewport.
- **Generalising the alpha-key fix to the multi-texture combiner path** — Legacy's discard lives inside its combiner and is gated on a can_discard flag that many pixel shaders never set, so those multi-texture alpha-key quads draw uncut there as well -- Unity already matches. Forcing _AlphaMode >= 1 for every alpha-key material would create a new divergence while closing an old one. Confine the fix to the single-texture path.
- **The legacy C++ defects the audit surfaced** — The 4-bit palettized alpha mask (Source/games/wow/Texture.cpp:248, `alpha = (*a & (0xf << cnt++)) * 0x11;`) and the sizeof-on-a-std::vector skin count (Source/games/wow/WoWModel.cpp:1499, `int numSkinFiles = sizeof(skinFileIDs);`) are real, but fixing them changes the reference picture in the middle of a parity programme. Record them and leave them until the Unity side is being judged against the game rather than against the viewport.
- **Unity's mip chain and filtering** — WmvModelBuilder.cs:1488-1492 builds and samples a mip chain; the legacy path never samples one. Unity is the better picture here, so this is a divergence to keep, not a parity gap to close by degrading it.
- **Shipping UV transforms without the STATIC bit and the env-unit exemption in the same branch** — A sphere map sliding across metal, or a body texture crawling on a batch the file marks static, reads as a camera or rigging bug and is more damaging than no animation at all. These three land together or not at all.

## Open questions, and the four that were closed

These are the questions the analysis flagged as unanswerable from the sources it read. Four of
the five were closed while writing this document -- three from the model sweep and one from the
Unity project that builds the player -- and are kept here with their answers rather than deleted,
because the reasoning that made each one look uncertain is worth seeing. One remains genuinely
open, and the diagnostic beside it is what would settle it.

- **Settled while writing this: the colour space is Linear.** The Unity project that actually builds `UnityRenderer.exe` declares `m_ActiveColorSpace: 1` (Linear; 0 would be Gamma) in its `ProjectSettings/ProjectSettings.asset`. The reason the audit could not find it is itself worth recording: that project lives OUTSIDE this repository, so the single setting that decides how every texture is decoded and every lit pixel is written is not version-controlled alongside the shaders and scripts that depend on it. Anyone rebuilding the player elsewhere can silently get the other answer. Worth fixing by committing the project settings, or at minimum by logging the active colour space at startup so a run says which one produced it.
- **Whether the 65535-index wrap actually fires on the models that matter here. The defect is certain (Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:80 vs :138); its incidence on the in-game reference models is not.** — Log the parsed triangle count and any submesh whose Level word is non-zero at Wow/M2SkinParser.cs:80, then load the four reference models. All-zero Levels means branch 1 is cheap insurance rather than an active fix, and it can be merged into branch 2 instead of leading.
- **Settled by the sweep: skeleton files are rare on creatures and common on player characters.** Of the 7,598 models swept, 122 carry a `SKID` chunk (1.6 %). Split by category that is 62 of 4,202 creatures (1.5 %) and 60 of 143 character models (42.0 %), and none at all among spells, items, world models, environments or particles. So the in-tree note (WmvModelBuilder.cs:338-340, one model in a spread of 300 creatures) is right and the domain report's "most creatures" is not. `SKID` stays where the roadmap puts it for creature work, and becomes a prerequisite rather than a nicety for the characters stage.
- **Settled by the sweep: the reference models do carry texture transforms.** The five models this project has been testing against declare 1 (the Lich King's unarmed variant), 4 (the void elemental behind the Essence of Shadow), 5 (`shaboss_doubt`), 7 (`ratmount2`) and 8 (`dragonelementium`) texture transforms respectively. The array is not empty on the models that matter, so the animated-material branch stays ahead of the effects branch. What the sweep cannot say is how many of those transforms MOVE rather than holding a constant offset; that needs the tracks themselves, which is one log line once the 0x60 array is parsed.
- **Not actually unknown: all ten domain reports arrived in full.** The ranking above was written by a final pass that summarises the ten reports, and that pass was handed a truncated copy of them, so it reported four as missing. They are not: the parity table in this document carries 42 rows for particles, 31 for ribbons and 28 for events, attachments, cameras and model lights, each with its own citations and its own verification pass. There was never a separate lighting or characters/equipment domain -- those were not among the ten commissioned. The consequence to keep in mind is narrower than it first reads: the RANKING of stages C and E rests on direct greps of this tree rather than on those reports, and the greps are quoted in the rows themselves.

## The diagnostic this audit added

`-m2inspect <list.txt> [out.csv]` — one model per line, as an internal path or `fdid:<id>`.
For each it reads the file's raw bytes, walks the chunks, reads the M2 header and reports the
counts that say what the model carries, plus which sibling chunks are present. It reads the
header only and never constructs a model, so it cannot be stopped by a model the full loader
chokes on, and a sweep of thousands of files takes about a minute.

```
wowmodelviewer.exe -dbfromfile -m2inspect models.txt out.csv
```

It is forensic and read-only: it writes only the report it is asked for. It is the tool that
produced every percentage and every validation model above, and re-running it is how those
numbers are checked rather than trusted.

## What this audit did not do

- No rendering behaviour was changed, and no value was tuned.
- Characters and equipment were left alone entirely.
- Model files that are encrypted in this client decode to nonsense; 29 of them were rejected
  by a sanity bound rather than silently averaged into the percentages.
- The sweep covers 8,009 of the client's models, sampled across every category, not all of
  them; the shares are of that sample.
